ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u5	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Witold Czarnecki, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u6	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present to the High House the position of my club in relation to the proposed law on the accessibility of digital websites and mobile applications of public entities, Seym printing Nos 3119 and 3216. The draft law adopted by the Commission on Digitalisation, Innovation and Modern Technology implements the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council and is part of the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States. The draft law is also part of the government's "Availability+' programme, whose main objective is to improve the quality of life and ensure the independence of citizens who, due to age, health status or disability encounters various kinds of difficulties in everyday life. The most important thing in the proposed law is that it ensures digital accessibility of public entities' websites and non-governmental organisations aimed at promoting people with disabilities, older people, as well as health assistance and protection. Persons with disabilities should be particularly concerned by the State, including the interest in accessing digital goods. Increasingly, the activity of the state and its institutions is moving to the digital world, and in this ongoing digital transformation, people with disabilities and older people must not be ignored. The draft law therefore counters social exclusion, which makes it a perfect part of the Europe 2020 strategy. The Act indicates the need to ensure the availability of a page, for example, in the form of such functionalities that a person who cannot use a computer mouse will be able to use the keyboard to move from one side to the other, from the bookmark to the bookmark, increase or decrease the font or increase contrast. This is a small technical challenge and how much needed in practice. But it's not just purely technical requirements that prove digital availability. The Act requires that the content itself be presented in an understandable manner. How many times, ladies and gentlemen, we have keyed the websites in search of information, going from bookmark to bookmark, from link to link, entering more and more looped appeals, and often, feeling deceived, we have left the site with nothing. Thus, the Act gives everyone the right to simple access to the pages and their contents. Of course, this is already happening, also in the administration, but it is necessary to step up work in this direction and this is what this law is doing. However, it should be stressed that the draft law at the same time offsets the need to ensure digital accessibility and the need for a proportionate burden on public entities and non-governmental organisations mentioned by me. The provisions of the Act indicate that where the availability of a digital website or material contained therein would be disproportionate to the expenditure, this availability may be provided in an alternative manner. Sometimes it is enough to read to someone interested in its content over the phone, and sometimes it is enough to send it a file in a friendly format, e.g. to the e-mail address. In the case of deaf people it is possible to provide contact by means of a sign language translator via electronic communication. The Act does not restrict the catalogue of alternative forms of access. It is important that an elderly or disabled person can get acquainted with the content. High Seym! To conclude and sum up my speech, I would like to say that my parliamentary club supports the bill submitted, which has been delivered to ladies and gentlemen in Seym Papers Nos 3119 and 3216. At the same time, I am placing on your hands two amendments tabled by the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. Thank you so much for your attention. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u7	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mr Krystian Jarubas, the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u8	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I am pleased to present my position towards the government's draft law on the accessibility of digital websites and mobile applications of public entities, prints no. 3119 and 3216. Ladies and gentlemen! The draft law is the implementation of the requirements of the European Union. The aim is to implement into the Polish legal order Directive 2016/2102 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 October 2016 on the accessibility of websites and mobile applications of public sector bodies. The Internet is a common good. Today, we are also facing challenges in the fight against digital exclusion. Especially in this case, this applies to people with disabilities and dysfunctions. According to official data, there are about 5 million people living in Poland, which represents more than 12% population. Ladies and gentlemen! According to regulations adopted by 2020 at the latest, government and local government websites are expected to reach full availability. This principle of accessibility should be understood as principles and techniques that should be applied when designing, creating, maintaining and updating websites and mobile applications so that they are more accessible to users. The regulation introduces rules of action for administrations. First of all, the most important is the information provided by the published document and not the document itself, so in the case of unavailable files the citizen will be entitled to call or send an email and ask for them to be read. The information that has legal implications must be absolutely accessible, i.e. that information should be adapted in the first place. The principle of feedback is also introduced: the first rule of access to all information published by the Office, e.g. a body publishing content on social media that does not guarantee technical accessibility will have to publish a copy of this information on its website. Ladies and gentlemen! The first step for the office in fulfilling its accessibility obligation is to create a declaration of accessibility of a website that will guide the disabled to move on the office website. Therefore, the club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats will support the regulations introduced. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u12	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Directive aims to achieve the objective of increasing the accessibility of websites and mobile applications of public sector bodies based on common accessibility requirements. As a result, citizens will benefit from greater access to services and information provided by public sector bodies through the possibility of ensuring more complete and more effective accessibility of digital websites and mobile applications. It should be stressed here that the proposed changes in accordance with the spirit and letter of the implementing directive are also intended, which is very important, to equalise the opportunities of people with disabilities in the use of public entities' websites and applications. The area covered by the proposed legislation is currently subject to legal regulations in Poland, partly at the statutory level - says the Act of 17 February 2005 on the informationization of activities of entities carrying out public tasks, and partly at the level of implementing acts, i.e. regulations. However, as the studies show, only 50% public entities comply with the requirements of the above-mentioned regulations. There is therefore no doubt about the need to introduce national laws which implement the above-mentioned Directive. However, attention should be paid to the need to modify certain provisions of the proposed bill, and in particular the scope of the regulated entities - says the article. 2 (4) - so as not to create an additional catalogue of entities that goes beyond the requirements laid down in the Directive. This aspect, in my opinion, rightly draws the attention of the Polish Districts Association in the opinion sent to the chairman of the committee. It also seems too overzealous not to take advantage of the possibility offered by the directive to exempt schools from these obligations, for which under Polish conditions the costs of implementing these provisions are quite high. I am convinced that it is better to increase the funding of teachers' salaries than to impose more costly responsibilities on schools. Although the Minister explained that it would be possible for the municipalities to take over these tasks from schools, I did not find specific records on this subject. I hope it's gonna be in some kind of amendment. The proposal also introduces an obligation to draw up a declaration of availability, which goes beyond the mandatory framework of the Directive and, in practice, the extended scope of information contained in the declaration will unfortunately result in its illegibility. In this respect, the European Commission, within the framework of its powers, adopted an implementing decision of 11 October 2018, establishing a model accessibility statement in accordance with the Directive. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u24	Dear Marshal! Dear Members, First of all, I would like to thank you for the very smooth process on the bill at the first reading at the committee meeting on Monday. Thank you very much for the factual and substantive, long, exhaustive discussion that has brought much and explained a lot. This project had to be discussed for a long time, because it is an important and good project. As we have said, as has already been said here, this is a project, which is the first draft law under the "Availability+" programme. As of today, there is a regulation on the National Interoperability Frameworks, a regulation on WCAG, but only less than 50% of public entities' websites apply to these regulations. That's why this bill is so important. For those of us who are lucky not to live and not function with disabilities, it is more difficult to imagine the life of those who on a daily basis in a different reality because of their limitations must function. And here I find it very difficult to agree with what the Members have said above all, that is, that the bill is, however, oversized. Ladies and gentlemen, we must take into account the limitations and disabilities of those who function in our society. As it has been said here today, these people are quite a few, and we must meet them. And that's why there's this bill. The data was dropping today. And it is so important that today, however, we take into account their limitations. Now I'm gonna answer the questions one at a time. Mr Wilczyński also raised the issue of over-dimension of the law. He said that this bill was prepared without cost awareness. He also asked what excessive costs were. Here I would like to draw attention to the fact that we have actually done these analyses, we have said how much it will cost to adapt the parties. Of course, it all depends on how extensive the website is. On the other hand, the Ministry of Digitalization will provide all those who run such websites, not only templates, but also e-learning training and webinars, thanks to which website editors will learn what to do. These are not primarily IT activities, they are largely editorial activities, which will allow people with disabilities to move on these sites and have access to all information. There have been many words about declarations that are supposed to be excessive. Mr Masłowski also spoke about this. You said that we have come up with some additional obstacles, restrictions that we impose on the operators of these sites. This is not true, because the directive, yes, has a narrower catalogue, while the implementing decision that has been issued for this directive already provides for a much broader one. And in fact in this executive decision we have mandatory requirements and optional requirements and we in the draft law, after a long consultation with the social side, after a long consultation with persons with disabilities, have decided that we will actually include this information as mandatory in the declaration of availability. But is it really information that is so burdensome to enter? Please pay attention to the content of the article. Article 10 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 (6). The following additional information is provided: information on the availability of the public entity's architectural premises for disabled persons and information on the accessibility of the sign language translator via electronic communication. Are these really so far-reaching, additional requirements that result in re-formation? No, these are just two additional information, whose inclusion in this declaration costs little to the person who prepares the declaration, while for people with disabilities they are really crucial. You have also raised the issue here, the subject matter of the subject matter of the law and small associations, which may have negative consequences of having to adapt their parties. The question was asked, also by Mr Wilczyński, what are excessive costs. Excessive costs are those costs which, if incurred, would significantly impede the functioning of the organisation concerned. However, please also pay attention to the very content of the bill that says that if you... Mr Jachnik gave here an example of his association. Please pay attention to Art. 3 (1) (a) Point 2, paragraph 8, which states that the Act does not apply to the content not used to carry out the current tasks of a public entity and not updated or submitted after 23 September 2019 to reconstructions. Moreover, in the bill we made a fairly long deadline for adapting these pages, because it is 2023. There were also positions on Article. 7 and alternative possibilities to provide access to digital content. Please note that in the article. In paragraph 7, the first paragraph is replaced by the following: The 2 directory we created is an open directory. In particular, there will certainly be other opportunities that will allow public entities to provide access to these content, which we have not been able to predict today. Once again, I assure you that, as amendments have been tabled, we will have an opportunity to discuss the scope of the subject matter law. We are declaring openness to the discussion here. I hope that we will be able to clarify a lot more, clarify our common positions, and we will work out a compromise, because this, as I said, is an important act. We must bear in mind those who function in a more difficult reality on a daily basis than we here in the Chamber, as I am looking at today, to a large extent have come to function. During the work on this draft law, during the work of the subcommittee as well as the joint committees, a number of substantive amendments and several dozen legislative and editorial amendments were made to the draft law. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u28	Dear Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! On the basis of the article. Article 118 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland of 2 April 1997 presents to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law amending certain laws in connection with ensuring the application of Regulation 2016/679, print No 3050. Regulation 2016/679 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data and on the free movement of such data and repealing Directive 95/46/EC, hereinafter referred to as the GDPR, shall apply from 25 May 2018. It aims to establish a legal order which in turn aims to ensure the effective application of GDPR rules. To this end, many other laws also needed to be amended. In view of their number, it was decided to do so in the draft law on the introduction of the Personal Data Protection Act. It is up to each Member State of the European Union to ensure its effective application in its legal order by adopting the relevant internal rules. The main objective of the bill is to adapt the Polish legal order to GDPR, mainly by deleting provisions that are contrary to GDPR or which duplicate GDPR solutions. The draft law examines the need to regulate the catalogue of data at the statutory level, where the processing of personal data has been addressed, for example. the keeping of public registers. It has also been established whether the task laid down in the law generally applicable in a precise and unambiguous manner can be derived from the need for the processing of specific personal data. Where the task cannot be interpreted as meaning what personal data are processed during its execution, it appears necessary to supplement the substantive provision. The project also includes solutions that respond to a large number of actions aimed at using the personal data protection reform to mislead data controllers. It shall act against possible cases where information containing false information on the legal need for action has been sent to them. The proposal therefore proposes to amend the Penal Code by judging that the threat of unlawful prosecution is not only a threat to criminal proceedings, but also another procedure in which an administrative penalty may be imposed. One of the most important amendments introduced by the GDPR law refers to the Code of Administrative Procedure. It is an information obligation which the public administration will be able to implement in the first letter replying to the request. The draft provides that the office, after receiving the letter and preparing the reply, should send it together with a letter informing about the processing and protection of personal data, together with deadlines and other important information, for example. a judicial instruction on the time limits for the lodging of appeals. Such ones, among others. the arrangements provide for a draft law amending certain laws in order to ensure the application of Regulation 2016/679, known as GDPR. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I recommend the adoption of a bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u30	Dear Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! Joint Commissions: Administration and Home Affairs and Justice and Human Rights leaned on the report on the government draft law adapting the Polish legal order to the GDPR, among others. by deleting provisions which are contrary to the GDPR or which GDPR duplicate. Already at the outset, it is necessary to emphasize the fact that the procedated law of the Polish Parliament amends the existing wording of 167 laws in the relevant parts. Hence, government submission can undoubtedly be called as necessary and difficult at the same time. At this point, it is appropriate to stress the fact that the amendment of individual laws will entail the imposition of rights and obligations on the authorities of the State, as well as to a large extent affecting individuals. In view of the number and nature of the amendments necessary for the introduction into the Polish legal order to comply with Regulation 2016/679 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016, it was decided to make them in a single legal act. The draft law also introduces provisions which constitute restrictions on GDPR and which have been introduced in accordance with Article. 23. They were introduced only to the extent necessary for the proper performance of tasks by the data controllers. As a few examples of a general nature, it is necessary to specify at the statutory level, and in a precise, closed catalogue of processed data, which already arises from constitutional standards. The legislator shall indicate situations in which a separate data directory should not be created as it results from the task itself. Where the task cannot be interpreted as meaning what personal data are processed during its execution, it is necessary to supplement the material provision in order to ensure that the processing of precisely defined data can be carried out; as an example, I give the requirements to be met by the applicant. With regard to the processing of personal data in administrative proceedings, it was considered excessive and not intended to create such regulations in sectoral legislation which empower the investigating authority to process personal data already in the course of this procedure. The proposed amendments to the provisions of the Code of Administrative Procedure are intended to implement the Article. 86 GDPR, as well as regulating the disclosure of personal data in accordance with Union law. In this case, too, the powers of the complainants in relation to the processing of their personal data have been specified. A separate provision regulates the activities of special services for which the special provisions will not apply. In particular, the functioning of the petition law has been examined and it has been specified that the provisions of the Code of Administrative Procedure will apply to the area not regulated by that law. The legislator guaranteed the right of access to an independent and impartial court established by law, which in relation to the judiciary of the Republic of Poland means directly access of the citizen to the court. The Enforcement Act in the administration provides for obligations for certain persons to keep data confidential. The processing of personal data in the State Fire Department will concern candidates for service and present and former firefighters, which has been subjected to the GDPR regime, indicating that the controllers of the data of these persons will be all the managers of the organizational units of the State Firefight. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u46	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Road Transport Act. The proposed amendment to the Road Transport Act of 6 September 2001 results from Poland's need to comply with the obligations imposed by Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) laying down common rules for the interconnection of national electronic registers of road transport operators. This project aims to ensure the availability of relevant data in the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs, known as KREPTD, kept by the Chief Road Transport Inspector, for all competent authorities of other Member States. The proposed law amends the provisions on the possibility of transmitting the specific data referred to in the Annexes to the revised Regulation of the European Union to the system. The draft law also provides for provisions aimed at clarifying the current rules relating to the keeping of the national electronic register and ensuring its proper functioning. The provisions of the proposed law will apply only to events occurring after the entry into force of that act, therefore data on registration numbers and countries of registration of vehicles authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator, notified by the trader after obtaining such authorisation in the list will be transmitted to the national register by the obliged undertaking in this case to that undertaking and will report those data after the date of entry into force of the proposed law to any economic operator. Similar data on the status and type of Community licence or the number of vehicles notified to the licence will be transmitted to the register of operators authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator for whom inability to manage transport operations has been established and will be collected when the decision to establish the manager's inability to manage transport operations becomes final after the date of entry into force of the Act in question. Information on registration numbers and vehicle registration countries will be transmitted to the register of the information system operating in the Central Road Transport Inspector when the main inspector issues an authorisation to pursue the occupation of road transport operator. If the Starost is authorised, the data shall be transmitted to the national register by that Starost. Taking into account some of the amendments that I have presented, both the nature of the law itself adapting the law to the regulations of the European Union and the content of the rules, the Law and Justice Club welcomes the effects of the law and supports this draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

During the committee meeting we explained in a way, I think, quite unambiguously, that the law does not impose updating obligations on entrepreneurs. Of course, it imposes certain obligations on the public, while obligations concerning the increased scope of reported data relate to new entries. Now, back to the beginning, I'd like to say that transport businessmen are our eyeballs. Their interests are in no way affected by the 3-month vacathio legis. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u62	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! On behalf of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, I present a report on the parliamentary draft law on the Research Network of Łukasiewicz. The draft is contained in print No 3114 and the report of the joint committees is published in print No 3227. The project entered the Sejm on 10 December 2018. The Sejm at its 76th meeting on 17 January 2018. pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm addressed the above draft law to the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development for its consideration. The main objective of this project is to increase the level of research carried out and thus facilitate cooperation between science and industry, as well as to profile the activities of institutes in terms of applied research and implementation, which is intended to stimulate economic development. The direct objective is to create from the ground up, through institutional transformations, an efficient, extensive organisation for conducting and commercialising applied and industrial research and implementing the economic policy of the government. During the examination of the draft law, 18 substantive amendments and 8 amendments were adopted, which do not change the substance of the draft, because they are legislative and editorial in nature. The Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on the Economy ask for the report and recommend the adoption of a draft law on the Research Network of Łukasiewicz. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u63	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u64	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Poland's development needs, in particular those related to the need to increase the level of innovation of the economy, require the creation of an effective research facility, which will work on the most necessary economy and society's technologies. Members have taken the initiative of a systemic solution to meet the need for a strong research organisation, the Łukasiewicz Research Network, which will focus key technology institutes with recognised national and international potential. The primary objective of this organization will be to conduct research of key importance for the development of the Polish economy and science, which will translate into innovative solutions commercialised by industry. The network will set up 38 research institutes supervised by ministers of the economy, energy and digitalisation, which will operate in sectors with high innovative potential. The added value of the creation of the Łukasiewicz Research Network will be the effective coordination of actions undertaken so far at the level of individual institutes. The network will group 8,000. researchers and technical staff, who will often have unique competences on the scale of the industry. The Act creates a mechanism for combining these competences in the process of training staff for a modern economy through the possibility of extensive cooperation with other actors in the science sector in Poland, e.g. Colleges. It should be stressed that such activities are directly in line with the constitution for science already in force since October, where the aspect of good professional training of graduates for economic purposes is identified as one of the main missions of the higher education sector. The creation of the Łukasiewicz Research Network according to the proposed submission will also break one of the biggest problems of the Polish science sector, i.e. its fragmentation. Research activities will be monitored and evaluated by the network centre on an ongoing basis, which will allow for ongoing response to emerging potentials of new technological solutions, on the other hand, it will allow for rapid abandonment of projects that are not successful. This will not only contribute to a more rational management of public funding for science, but will also enable quick decisions to support new ideas that emerge much faster in innovative solutions than a normal administrative system is able to respond. To sum up, the proposed Law on the Research Network Łukasiewicz, in my opinion, in the assessment of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, is a golden measure combining administrative capacity to minimise market risk in the development of innovative solutions with the effectiveness of large economic organisations, which are most successful in a globalised economy. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u82	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! If you don't mind, I will start by answering Mrs Iwona Michalek's questions, supplemented by Mr Jerzy Meysztowicz, about this thread, which also appeared in other speeches - the number of institutes and the processes in which it was consulted. The proposed concept of the Łukasiewicz Research Network was consulted with all stakeholders. In the form of a parliamentary project, it was sent for monthly consultations, which were not shortened and included all the representatives of the institutes. So that the institutes could learn about this project. The results of this consultation were graciously quoted. We have the comments of the General Council of Science and Higher Education and the Conference of Rectors of Academic Schools of Poland. I would like to point out here that most of these are not opinions rejecting the project itself. These are opinions that relate to detailed doubts or questions, while they do not reject the very idea of establishing the Łukasiewicz Research Network. No other protests were brought in the context of this public consultation. Then what we can presume means that both the Council of the Central Research Institutes and other bodies representing the research institutes of this protest are missing. The criterion for joining the Łukasiewicz Research Network was the decision of the Minister's Supervisory Institutes. Ministers have for many years, not since this government's term of office, been overseeing individual institutes and carrying out their control and supervisory functions in relation to them, which we actually duplicate in this law, i.e. we do not change anything here. The competent minister, after consulting his directors of institutes, decided which of these institutes would be included in the Łukasiewicz Research Network, which means that the decision to join the Łukasiewicz Research Network was taken by the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology, and this is the vast majority of institutes, as well as the Ministry of Digitalization and the Ministry of Energy. It was a decision made by the ministers in consultation with the directors. All the directors of institutes who have been declared as being able to create the Łukasiewicz Research Network have been consulted on the detailed arrangements proposed in this Act. At least, I think we've met four times with all the directors. Any one of them could have made comments. I also pointed out at the meeting of the joint committees that some of the proposed amendments, which were tabled by the trade unions, were... In fact, we could agree with these proposals, but that would mean that we would have to withdraw the solutions that we introduced into the law as a result of a public consultation, in other words: we have taken into account those comments that the directors have submitted to us as more functional, and even if today another part of the environment would like to change them, we are already in these common arrangements. These 38 institutes represent very much different institutes. Of course, there are also those that have a slightly less optimal income structure, as we might expect from them. I allow myself to be slightly rhetorical... gentleness towards these institutes, because - this is very important - we, after a deeper analysis of institutes, came to the conclusion that it is not the art of closing institutes, the art is to exploit their potential. Very often these institutes, which are in a very difficult situation today, actually save their budget by renting office space. With a better governance structure and opportunities for use, e.g. from both staff and infrastructure resources of other institutes, they can start working much better. That is why we do not want to eliminate, close or stigmatise only institutes, but rather to combine them and use their potential in the right way. The network is, as Mrs Michalek asked, open. You'll be able to approach her. The decision will each time, as consistently from the beginning, belong to the competent minister supervising the competent institute. It is also a principle of network cooperation with external actors. As you have pointed out, we have a special provision that talks about the possibility of establishing partnerships, which is particularly important, with universities, because it responds to the problem that you have reported, the problem of degrees and academic titles. The Scientific Personnel in the Research Network Łukasiewicz will be able to develop its academic career paths, but in this case we would like to take advantage of the powers that universities will have. We think it's a mechanism. not only more attractive and effective, but also strengthening cooperation between universities, institutes. Are we dealing with any protests? This law was whether these solutions were consulted with the environment for so long, that I think we are all in a situation where we would like to be resolved, as if the consultation process itself, to be decided and verified, to verify how these mechanisms work, rather than to continue to discuss them only and exclusively. Mr Piotr Pyzik asked about the structure of employment. As a rule, new people will appear at the Research Network Centre of Łukasiewicz. This will be a very small centre, taking into account comparable French or German organisations. On the other hand, employment issues are regulated in accordance with labour law, employment issues in research institutes, research institutes of the Łukasiewicz Research Network will be subject to the same regulations that other institutes are subject to, i.e. labour law will not violate any requirements here. I understand that this is a bit of a question referring to how many scientific staff will be in the Łukasiewicz Research Network. In the first period this number will be the same as what it is now. On the other hand, any questions that concern such issues, my or our answer is always the same. The decisions will be made by the directors and the center. If policy is to be further shaped, it is not a question of the decision of the Ministry. And that's how I hope I answered all the questions. If I've left one of them out, I'm asking you to forgive me and, of course, we'll give you... [[Distribution of benefits...]] Distribution of benefits in the network itself. [[Yes.]] I mean, here's the rule. Oh, I know that, actually, one of you has been paying attention to the fact that the legal form is inconsistent, I think it was Mr Brynus that we have a network... The Centre of the Research Network Łukasiewicz is a state legal entity, and research institutes of the Łukasiewicz network have their own legal personality, not state personality. This is due to the fact that most of the budget of the Centre will be a public budget, because the Centre itself will not deal directly with commercialisation. And I'm referring here to the problem of distribution. The centre is not, as if working at its own expense, to work at the expense of institutes, i.e. to be such a classic support institution. On the other hand, as for the structure of the distribution of funds, which is our internal algorithm, this will be the subject of negotiations and discussions between all directors. So the directors of the College together with the future president of the Łukasiewicz Research Network will determine how the funds will be distributed online, and please note that these funds will consist of several levels. Level one is the grant that institutes are getting today and it is already being paid, which is the basic budget that is the budget of each institute. There are also funds that will be allocated to specific projects implemented within the network. And there will still be budgets for special tasks that will be built in consortia. Each time the decision will be a collegial decision, and he will manage the process of obtaining consent, this discussion will be moderated by the President. If you ask me if this is possible, I can tell you that in one of the companies we supervise, there are, for example, companies. investment panels introducing a culture in which none of the managers of an entity within the company can purchase any infrastructure or equipment unless they obtain the consent of all other managers. In this sense, the president, the director of this company, does not decide personally, but says: You must agree on what is really needed by our entire organization. We would like to move this model to a higher level in the future. As you can see, this is not a matter of centralization, it is simply a matter of coordination. Today, institutes don't have this mechanism. And the potential is great, and even during the previous parliamentary debate, I mentioned the first reading that in this respect we are proposing solutions that are similar or similar, I would draw attention to those that operate in Germany or France. We have VTT advisors, which is from a very efficient Finnish network. The experience of Finland is particularly important to us, because it is a country that has also made up for some kind of arrears. It has a much smaller structure compared to such a powerful organisation as Fraunhofer, and can be so effective that it ranks fourth in the ranking of those who make the best use of funds from Horizon 2020. So we take advantage of our experiences, we critically approach them and apply them wisely to our reality. Let me stress once again: the fact that only part of the institutes are part of the network, in my opinion, is an advantage. This can be seen as a practical attempt to verify whether commercialisation processes cannot be carried out in a different mode than they have been carried out so far - without the liquidation of earlier ones. If we have commercialisation in universities, research institutes that remain in the old regime and throughout the Łukasiewicz Research Network, we will be able to analyse and compare which of these solutions are most effective. Since I am pleased to be the representative of the Prime Minister for the reform of research institutes, I can, of course, stress on this occasion that all institutes are equally valuable to us and we will also support those research institutes which will not operate within the Łukasiewicz Research Network. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u86	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! A few words of introduction. The establishment of the Medical Research Agency was justified by the need to create institutional support for the organization and financing of analyses and research in health care, which will contribute to the innovation of Polish medicine. The Agency is to support the development of research in the field of medical sciences, health sciences and the development of medical technologies, including, what is very important, non-commercial clinical trials on medicinal products and medical devices. It is also intended to support epidemiological research and modelling on the basis of large public data collections in health care. All of the functions listed here and similar to those of the Medical Research Agency are important, but it seems that the most important function is to arrange, organise, coordinate and finance non-commercial clinical trials, namely clinical trials, which are not funded by pharmaceutical companies or companies operating on the medical devices market. Such public institutions, such as the Medical Research Agency, operate in many developed countries and have a moderate and inspiring impact on clinical research markets, including. by undertaking research which does not fall within the scope of concern. In many countries, they allow development potential to be built in the sphere of national health systems, increasing the revenues of public entities in these countries. The interesting thing is that some of these foreign institutions have signals of waiting for the appointment of a Polish counterpart, which will be the Agency for Medical Research. It must be remembered that the strength of the Polish health system is the medical staff with a high research potential and well developed research infrastructure. It is prepared to conduct high-tech research. In short, it's time to set up a medical research agency. The government project on this issue was slightly modified following work in the Sejm. The main changes are as follows. Firstly, the principle of appointment of the President of the Agency was introduced in the procedure of the competition procedure, while, of course, maintaining all the high requirements for the candidate that the project provided for. Secondly, it was decided explicitly, not by default, to transfer to the acting President of the Agency during a transitional period, i.e. in the period before the appointment of the President, his statutory powers, i.e. the statutory powers assigned to the Head of the Agency. Thirdly, a number of amendments have been made in order and legislative terms. Most of the votes of the Sejm Health Committee rejected the nine amendments tabled in the course of the work, which eventually became so-called. minority proposals. On behalf of the committee, I hope that on behalf of the entire committee, I am convinced that the law on the Agency for Medical Research needs to be adopted quickly. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u138	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Circle of Members of the Free and Solidarnia, I present my position towards the government's draft law on the Agency for Medical Research. During the work to date on this project, it has been repeated on several occasions that currently only 1% Research in Poland is non-commercial research, and companies, which is obvious and understandable and profit-oriented, conduct research in areas that are cost-effective for them. The establishment of an institution which, in carrying out its assigned tasks, will fulfil the current deficit of non-commercial clinical trials is therefore, in my opinion, an extremely valuable initiative. This draft law sets out rules for the functioning of the Agency for Medical Research, the main purpose of which will be to finance research and development projects in the field of medical sciences and health sciences, to provide opinions and expertise in favour of, among others. public administrations and the initiation and development of international cooperation. Solutions included in the project such as e.g. the appointment of the President of the Agency by means of a competition by the Minister for Health, the statutory empowerment of a number of competences which the candidate must have, or the obligation to prepare an annual report on the activities of the Agency, serve in my assessment to maintain the highest standards of its operation. I have no doubt that ABM, as a specialist institution dedicated to supporting and developing medical research in our country, will not only increase its number but will be crucial in disseminating access to the latest medical technologies. And easier access to innovation, better and more effective treatments are invaluable benefits for patients. Therefore, I do not think that there are entirely reasonable reservations raised with regard to the funding of the agency. According to the project, its activities are expected to be financed entirely from the State budget in the first year. In the following years, when the Agency's activities are already in effect, a part of the appropriations of 0,3% the planned revenue due on health insurance contributions will be transferred from the National Health Fund. This is the money that will be returned in the form of benefits to patients. It is also important that, thanks to the work of the government of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, there is an extraordinary increase in expenditure on the health care system, which in 2024 is as high as 6%. GDP. When we talk about financing health care, there will always be a lack of satisfaction, but with every next small step we are closer to the goal and it is in my opinion that we should be positive. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding this project.

The median, i.e. this distance line between Bornholm and the Polish coast, is the basic line adopted in the case law of international courts, in international case law and in international law as a dividing line between such areas as disputed areas. In the case of several countries, we had such judgments, e.g.: Denmark - Norway, Nicaragua - Colombia, but also Libya - Malta. In each of these disputes, the basis used first as a temporary measure known to us from other forums, and then in the final decision was always a median line without leaving, i.e. without division. Like us, unlike what we've negotiated. Secondly, in the case of the Baltic we had three situations, along with our similar nature. We had the issue of Sweden and the issue of Germany in relation to relations with Denmark, taking into account the particular situation of Bornholm. Now, unfortunately, I have to quote a few numbers. It is usually the case that this circumstance may be taken into account, with an emphasis on possibly, when making deviations from the median line is the so-called. the proportion of the length of the coast, i.e. in our case - the Polish coast and the Bornholm coast. In this case, which I have mentioned, Norway and Denmark, which is also the Baltic Sea, this proportion of the length of the coast was 9.2 to 1. In our case, in the case of Poland - 5.1. Similar distances, 8 or 8,2, 8,1, in the other cases I mentioned. The Act deals with a number of important health issues, such as improving the organisation of inspections carried out by the NFZ, covered very widely and in detail, organisational changes concerning night and Christmas aid and the principles of securing this assistance, the introduction of harmonised medical segregation for all entities, as well as amendments to the Act on State Medical Rescue, which include the introduction of a mechanism to ensure the rationalisation of human resources, but also others, as well as many other important problems. Five substantive amendments and 10 legislative amendments were introduced by the committee during and as a result of the discussion. The substantive amendments concerned the improvement of the implementation of scientific research by the Agency for the Evaluation of Medical Technologies and Tarification by including these studies as a statutory task. This will improve the work and improve the work, the agency will not have to sign further agreements with the Ministry of Health. With regard to the Agency for the Evaluation of Medical Technologies and Tarification, the amendment consisted in increasing the limits on the remuneration costs of the Agency's staff. The following amounts have been mentioned. Increasing these limits is essential for the proper performance of the Agency's tasks, as experience so far shows that these tasks generate high costs as high-quality experts need to be involved. It excluded from the cap on remuneration costs received in the framework of projects co-financed by EU funds and other projects. The next substantive amendment was to supplement the regulation on the management system for SOR patients, the processing of personal data. There was a need to supplement the rules because of the need to protect against abuse or unlawful access to data or the transfer of personal data. Another amendment also concerned the processing of personal data of recipients in order to account for the cost of reimbursement of medicines. It was about the National Health Fund. The Health Committee, after detailed examination and after voting on these amendments, asks the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to present my position on the draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare. The National Health Fund will finance this year the purchase of benefits and medicines for millions of Poles for over PLN 80 billion. This is the second largest stream of money that goes to society through state authorities after social benefits. Taking such a high level of responsibility, the National Health Fund must be sure that these funds are spent in an efficient and rational manner. In view of the above, the Minister for Health has prepared a draft law whose main objective is to improve the effectiveness of the checks carried out by the National Health Fund. More than 2 000 of them are carried out each year as part of the Fund's activities. The proposed solutions should allow the desired results to be achieved through a two-track approach. Firstly, control will become more effective, among others. thanks to the centralization of all control cells, which are today scattered in 16 voivodships, as well as through the creation of a professional control corps, to which the best and carefully selected employees will reach. The higher requirements imposed on controllers will also allow them to be given additional responsibility for self-control and post-control. The best practices already applied by the National Tax Administration and the Social Insurance Institution have been used in strengthening the control institution. The reinforcement of the fund's control units will be combined with the implementation of the National Health Fund's previously absent activities in practice, such as the prohibition of simultaneous control by the Minister and the Fund, the prohibition of re-control of the same scope, the limit of working days during which control may be carried out in a given calendar year, and the notifications of checks transmitted seven days in advance. In addition, there will be an obligation to document in the form of photographs or recordings of irregularities which have been found in the course of an inspection. In order to reduce the costs incurred by providers and the fund, the exchange of documents will take place in electronic form. It is worth noting that the last two solutions are a novelty in the state control institutions and that if they meet their hopes, they should be introduced in other administrative departments. The principle pursued by the project developers was to enable efficient and consistent action towards the strong and understanding of the smaller ones. This is reflected in the introduction of the threshold of triviality. Penalties below 500 PLN will not be investigated by a fund whose control services will focus on major incidents and expose the payer to real losses. In addition to changes in control, the Act introduces solutions to improve patients' access to emergency benefits. National hospitals, which have an SOR or an emergency room, will be able to provide night and Christmas medical care after the application has been submitted. At the same time, the SOR activities will be improved by introducing uniform national rules on medical segregation. This will be complemented by the implementation of the patient care system financed by the Minister of Health, which includes: will show the expected waiting time for assistance. The project also includes a major change for hundreds of hospitals that are currently online. As a basis for the calculation of the lump sum, the entire period of 2018 will be considered, and not only its second half, which is a more favourable solution for most individuals, as the second half of the year consists of two months of holidays and a December holiday break. High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I recommend that the Sejm adopt a draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u192	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club to present a position on the draft law amending the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds and certain other laws. As the name suggests, the project contains changes that are in a number of changes relevant to the healthcare system. It concerns changes in the scope of as many as six laws: the Act on the provision of health care financed by public funds, the Act on Pharmacy Chambers, the Law on Pharmaceutical Law, the Act on State Medical Rescue, the Act on the disclosure of documents of safety authorities, the Act on the refund of medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses and medical devices. This is a very wide range of changes, which is why as members of our club we tried to ask for a subcommittee to deal with such broad legislation. We also tried to propose a change in the procession to move certain laws to a separate procession. We didn't get approval for this. Therefore, in this huge range of changes practically 4/5 concerns the creation of an inspection corps by the President of the National Health Fund. This means that we are back to centralization, because the auditors have also carried out these checks since the start of the health fund in the voivodship branches of the fund. Centralisation brings about a return to top-down management and liquidation of these departments. The employees of the voivodship branches who were in charge of control will be directly subject to the President of the National Health Fund by virtue of the law for the next two years. The auditors who want to continue this function will have to take their exams after 2 years. However, this applies only to those who are head office employees, and therefore new people who wish to enter the competition will not have the opportunity. In addition, auditors will also assess the quality of medical services. There is no requirement among controllers that these are persons who have medical education, therefore it is necessary to call into question the quality of medical services by controllers who do not have medical education. The duration of the check shall be limited for each establishment within a single 12-month calendar dimension. The bill states that this check will not be duplicated, but we know perfectly well that the Minister of Health at any time, at any stage of the procedure can go to medical entities with personal control, so this possibility of duplication will unfortunately exist. There is also no provision in this Act concerning the costs to be incurred in the performance of the functions of controllers. We do not know whether the costs associated with the establishment of this corps will translate into a reduction in the amount of funding for medicinal activities. I think it's just one of the elements. Another element of the amendment of these laws is the introduction of the possibility of providing night and Christmas assistance by national state hospitals. We are returning to this pier that was already in operation in the 1960s and 1970s. in health care, where within a single large health establishment all operators were actually active. I think that the possibility of introducing this night and Christmas care is intended not only to improve the quality of the services provided by doctors, but also to relieve the burden of emergency rooms and hospital units burdened by excess patients and queues. Therefore, there is no care about the quality of the benefits, the aim is to shorten the queues in reception rooms and SORs. Of course, there are provisions in the Act relating to the SORs, which we support and support during the work in committee. They talk about patient handling, so-called. TOPSOR, medical segregation and how patients in the SOR will expect medical benefits. Five categories of waiting time are introduced: from immediate to very shortened. There is also a provision in the Act concerning the extension of the definition of a medical emergency medicine, since by 2020 we were to be treated only by doctors of this system. There are also sharp and out of focus on duty. We were also talking about the validity of these records and we were in favour of some of these changes, but because [[♪ The bell ♪]] they are so broad and inconsistent, and we support only some of them. As a number of these records cause chaos in the bill, we will abstain from voting. [[Applause]]

At the same time, we introduce very strict reservations about who might be the controller, in which situations the controller should itself raise doubts, e.g. related to the fact that he is in any way family-related or has a permanent relationship with the person being controlled or with the employee of the person being controlled. We introduce a ban on work for a controlled entity for 2 years after the inspection has been completed and, what is important, we shift responsibility for maintenance, not on the controller himself, but on the person who has been subject to control, which is to say: Dear witness, it is your duty to ensure, under the threat of fine or detention, that the person who has carried out the inspection of your facility within 2 years of the completion of this inspection is not employed in it. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u229	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Krzysztof Szulowski, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u230	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club vis-à-vis the government's draft amendment to the Health Protection Act of 9 November 1995 against the consequences of the use of tobacco and tobacco products, prints Nos 3201 and 3244. The amendment aims at adapting the existing provisions to the solutions adopted in the Commission's implementing decisions on the establishment of a common format for the notification of electronic cigarettes and spare containers, the format for the transmission and sharing of information on tobacco products, the definition of micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. In addition, the amendment clarifies the existing legislation on the production, presentation and sale of tobacco products and related products. The Health Protection Act provides for a number of obligations which have been imposed on the chemical inspector and clarifies the provisions on the performance of the inspector's tasks. It was proposed, among others. in the proceedings conducted by the inspector, the introduction of specific provisions on service procedures with the competent authorities of the Member States of the European Union, international organisations and institutions of the European Union in electronic form without the need for a qualified electronic signature or a trusted signature, to an address made available in the register of the Joint Electronic Data Transmission Point, so-called. EUCEG to simplify procedures and facilitate communication with foreign entities. The parties were allowed to submit explanations in the English language of the inspector's proceedings to request the submission of letters translated into Polish. The powers of the inspector to establish the non-compliance of the submitted documentation concerning tobacco products, electronic cigarettes and spare containers with the requirements of the Act have been introduced. This amendment aims to introduce a procedure to ensure that only products complying with the requirements of the Act are placed on the market. Recommendations have been made for the Directive to refer to the definition of micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. Cases where the information provided is not considered to be a trade secret have been identified in order to remove the interpretative doubts concerning the information covered or not by the company's secret. In view of the need to ensure compliance of cigarettes with the requirements of the Act regarding maximum tar, nicotine and carbon monoxide levels in smoke, it was possible for the competent authorities of the State Sanitary Inspection to carry out the sampling of cigarettes. It has been clarified that the authorisation of an innovative tobacco product shall be made by decision in order to remove the interpretative doubts as to the form in which the inspector decides. In accordance with European Union legislation, the technical requirements for the unique identifier, registration, transmission, processing and storage of data and access to stored data have been standardised, the technical requirements for ensuring full compliance of systems used for unique identifiers and functions associated with other systems in the European Union. The requirements for the submission of electronic cigarettes and spare containers are clarified by indicating the obligation for each importer to make them where the manufacturer has not done so, and the obligations and powers of the contact person are specified. The expected effect of these measures will be to increase the effectiveness of the inspector's actions aimed at ensuring that tobacco products and related products, namely electronic cigarettes, spare containers and herbal products, comply with the requirements of the Act, are placed on the market. To date, the proposed amendments have proved ineffective or incompatible with the solutions adopted in the European Union. In the assessment of the Law and Justice Club, the proposed amendments are beneficial, in certain areas simplifying procedures to facilitate the conduct of business, increasing the inspector's control capacity, reducing illicit trade in tobacco products and thereby improving public health safety related to the use of tobacco, tobacco products and related products. The Law and Justice Club will support the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Among the most dangerous consequences of smoking are the increased risk of cardiovascular disease, malignancies, respiratory diseases, infertility and pregnancy disorders, damage to the immune system or general deterioration of health. Tobacco as a psychoactive substance also has strong addictive potential and has consequences in the area of mental health - it affects the central nervous system, is responsible for the onset of physical addiction and acts as a stimulant. There is still a lack of complete data describing the use of so-called. e-cigarettes. However, it can be assumed that their consumption is increasing and is becoming more and more popular among young people. The adoption of the amendments will slightly increase costs for the tobacco industry and consequently also for individual users. Without precise data in this area, however, it can be assumed that the changes made will reduce consumption and will have a positive impact on the health of Poles and Poles, with particular emphasis on young people. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u254	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to thank the representatives of all the clubs very warmly for their support for this project. During the discussion on this project, a number of questions were raised or doubts about the procedure for introducing this regulation. I would like to clarify that these provisions of the European Commission, this Directive, were established in 2014, but the implementing rules, the implementing regulation, which defined precisely how this Directive was implemented in the legal systems of the individual countries of the European Union, were only issued in April last year. Immediately after the publication of this regulation, the current government, the Ministry of Health, took concrete action to introduce these solutions in our country. Discussions took place in different environments. There have been proposals to extend this regulation to include provisions which are not directly derived from the Directive, and this has affected the extension of the negotiation process in connection with this project with the environments concerned by bringing it into legal order. The final decision of the permanent committee of the current government was that this project should contain only and exclusively provisions which result directly from the Directive of the European Union. Mr Kozłowski pointed out that too late the preparation of this project could prevent it from actually being introduced since 20 May 2019. I would like to stress that in the law and in the implementing regulation to this draft law, there is a clear indication that all products produced before that date and which appear on the market after 20 May do not have to be subject to the provisions and rigors of that law. So they can function in parallel on the market after 20 May. This period is defined as an annual period, i.e. until 20 May 2020. With regard to the exclusion of criminal liability for non-compliance with the provisions of this regulation, this is not possible because these provisions result directly from the EU Directive and the Regulation, so here the European Union does not leave the Member States of the European Union the freedom to apply the provisions on imposing or not penalties for failing to comply with these requirements. In the course of the discussion, questions were raised regarding problems related to the prevention of respiratory diseases or actions promoting a healthy lifestyle. Of course, such programmes are implemented both under the National Health Programme and other programmes financed by the Ministry of Health budget, they are also financed by other programmes implemented under other departments. It is implemented and implemented, with specific financial resources being allocated to it, which are a prerequisite for its smooth implementation. As regards the other activities of the Ministry of Health in the field of health care of Poles, prevention and promotional activities, they are carried out within the framework of the National Health Programme and among others. In the framework of this programme, the oncology centre has set up a clinic to help smokers. As part of this programme, we also provide training for medical personnel, equip them with appropriate knowledge, skills to effectively conduct preventive, promotional activities, and support those who are addicted to tobacco. We also run an action on a wider forum, among others. we cooperate with the Employers of the Republic of Poland through various kinds of actions, among others. free zones are created without smoke in workplaces. These are concrete examples of what the Minister of Health is doing. We can prepare written information for you. There is much more to this and I think we can document the specific broad actions of the Minister of Health in the field of care for the health of Poles. Mr Gonciusz asked about the scope of the data which will not be regarded as a trade secret, the data concerning the composition of individual products, as well as the thresholds which have been defined as thresholds which allow for the recognition that this does not constitute a trade secret. So here, as far as the possibility of affecting this phenomenon is concerned, the Polish Government does not have such an impact, because the implementing regulation specifically states what we may consider to be a trade secret in this case and what we do not. Therefore, the Polish Government has no freedom here, and we could not include such entries in the bill. So that I very much thank you all for your support at this stage of the process and ask you to adopt this law. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u260	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a position regarding the government draft law on the preparation and implementation of strategic investments in the oil sector, print No 3198. The revised draft law on the preparation and implementation of strategic investments in the oil sector ensures the creation of a legal framework allowing government policies to strengthen the energy security of the country. In order to strengthen this security, it is necessary to carry out a number of investments in the construction of pipelines for the transport of crude oil or petroleum products and the reconstruction, expansion or reconstruction of existing oil pipelines or petroleum products. The implementation of these necessary investments on the basis of the current general rules governing the investment process would imply the need to carry out a multi-annual process of preparation of investments, which would have a negative impact on the energy security of the Republic of Poland. In addition, note It is appropriate that the general provisions in force are not adapted to the preparation and implementation of complex linear investments, which are strategic investments in the oil sector. In order to enable efficient preparation and implementation of the above mentioned. investment in the draft law provides for a specific legal framework for preparation and implementation. The draft law provides for a number of simplifications in the procedure for preparing and implementing strategic investments consisting in particular: the introduction into the legal system, issued by the competent voivode, the decision to establish the strategic location of investments in the oil sector, which will be an instrument which will fully regulate the location of investments and their individual parts, which will lead to a minimization of the number of proceedings and the time taken to issue administrative acts necessary for the implementation of the investment; the simplification of the procedure for obtaining decisions prior to the determination of the strategic location of investments in the oil sector and water law; the acceleration of administrative proceedings by specifying the time for the administrative authorities to issue individual decisions and the introduction of a legal basis for imposing penalties in the event of a timely examination of the case; the introduction of provisions allowing the investor to effectively obtain the right to property necessary for the implementation of the investment, while ensuring adequate compensation for expropriation or compensation for the limitation of property rights; the possibility of an investor to enter the property premises for the purpose of carrying out measurements, studies or other work necessary for drawing up an information sheet of an undertaking or a report on the environmental impact of an undertaking; the application of the spatial planning and development of the law, the law, the law on the restoration of the restoration of the restoration of the law on the property and the agricultural and forestry sector. It must be stressed that the specific procedure for preparation and implementation concerns only the investments explicitly listed in the annex to the draft law. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed solutions contained in the draft law comprehensively regulate the investment process with regard to strategic investments in the oil sector, which will certainly simplify and streamline this process. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I ask the High House to adopt this bill. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

As regards the improvement in the situation of the refinery as a result of the construction of the second thread of the oil pipeline from Naftoport to Płock, of course the capacity in this section will be increased. At the moment the situation is that on the section from the Polish border to Płock there are three threads, on the section from Płock to the western border there are two threads, and on the section from Płock to Gdansk or from Gdansk to Płock there is one thread. This means that if necessary we are able to meet the needs of the refinery in Płock, but also the customers in Germany, but we are not able to meet all the requirements of customers who need raw material. Why? Because we are not able to transmit different kinds of oil, which in modern installations are necessary to optimize the entire refining process. This second thread, which we will build, will mean, first of all, that we are safe, because the failure, so to speak, on this one existing thread of Gdańsk - Płock would be a big problem. So let's go, I said this during the committee meeting, by way of small steps. There are special laws that are currently being applied. This is another special bill. Each next one collects the previous one's experience. That's what we've done, recently updating the terminal bill. Also in this law, which is a new law, we include these experiences which result from the application of previous ones. It is the intention that in the future these experiences can be used to simply normalize all of this, whether it's in a separate bill or in a code. Anyway, this is ahead of us. I can add, in conclusion, that this law that we are now investigating has a limitation, i.e. it is not a general law, but it has an annex, enumeratively mentioned investments. This is a kind of limitation, but also flexibility. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u286	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour of expressing my views on the draft law submitted to the Senate Parliamentary Sejm amending the Construction Law Act from prints No. 3195 and 3209. The aim of the project is to align the right with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 7 February 2018, which ruled that Article. 16 point 3 of the Law of 7 July 1994. Construction law, in so far as it authorises the competent minister to determine the limitation of the scope of building rights in the relevant regulation, is incompatible with Article. Article 65 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1st Constitution. Article 4 is replaced by the following: Article 65 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Constitution provides that freedom of choice and occupation and the choice of place of work are guaranteed for everyone. The exceptions are laid down in the Act. Art. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 the Constitution states that restrictions on the exercise of constitutional freedoms and rights can only be laid down in the Act and only if they are necessary in a democratic state for its security or public order or for the protection of the environment, health and public morality or freedom and rights of others. Such restrictions shall not affect the essence of freedom and rights. In the opinion of the Constitutional Court, as set out in Article. Article 65 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution freedom of work can be limited only in the act of the rank of law. Currently, the Construction Law defines among others. functions in construction, which can only be performed by persons with appropriate technical education and professional practice, established by a decision hereinafter referred to as building rights, issued by a professional self-government body. The Act regulates the specialties in which construction rights are granted, as well as the education and practice required for a particular type of speciality for the design or management of construction works without limitation or to a limited extent. The Act also provides that restrictions on the scope of building rights will be determined by regulation by the Minister responsible for construction, planning, spatial planning and housing in agreement with the Minister responsible for higher education and science. The essence of the proposed amendments is to raise to the rank of the law governing building rights by transferring the provisions of Chapter 4 of the Regulation of the Minister of Infrastructure and Development of 11 September 2014 on the independent technical functions in construction, governing the scope of building rights in § 10 Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In our opinion, the proposed amendments fully implement the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, which is why the Law and Justice Club will support the Senate draft law amending the Construction Law Act. Thank you.
