ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u362	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! The Government's draft law amending the law on support for rural development with the contribution of the European Agricultural Fund for Rural Development under the Rural Development Programme 2014 and some other laws introduces guarantees for loans that farmers and micro and small enterprises in the agri-food processing sector will be able to benefit from. At this moment, funds from the Rural Development Programme for the period 2014 to 2020 shall be granted in the form of grants which require own contribution and, in the form of non-refundable premiums. On the other hand, the project introduces a reverse financial instrument. As the government confirms, money for agriculture from the EU budget after 2020 will be smaller for Poland, which will result in increasing obstacles to obtaining grants. Therefore, the return on financial instruments, i.e. loans, should be more accessible. RDP loans are more favourable than ordinary loans, but these are still further steps to increase debt among farmers. In accordance with the arrangements with the European Commission, the revision of the Rural Development Programme for the period 2014 to 2020 concerning new forms of allocation of funds, such as returnable financial instruments, also requires changes in national law. As a result of these agreements, we are proceeding today on this project. However, it is important to consider how decisions made today will affect the debt of farmers in Poland. According to the National Debt Register of the Economic Information Office, the debt in the agricultural sector amounts to PLN 412 million and the average debt of the farmer is PLN 49 000. zł. The instability of the agricultural sector and the resulting problems faced by indebted farmers are mainly due to drought, low buying-in prices for certain products, long waiting times for payment of processed products and, of course, African swine fever problems. Within the framework of the Rural Development Programme, the possibility for a farmer to apply for a loan covered by a guarantee from a financial instrument is to be created. Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego is to secure loans to farmers who want to invest but who do not have their own collateral through agreements with banks lending farmers. Nevertheless, it aims to reduce subsidies while increasing debt. This is certainly not an arrangement which alone represents benefits for farmers. In order for this system to work in the longer term, it is necessary to support farmers more than previously in tackling the problems caused by weather disasters, other unforeseen external factors. As we know at this moment, this is not very successful, especially with African swine fever, we all know that this is a serious disaster. The government project should go through the public consultation process, and in this case the process was missing. Do we know what farmers think about this? No, because they will be faced with the fact that, in this case, the government did not even intend to know the opinions of farmers and entrepreneurs, because decisions were already made at the stage of consultation and agreement with the European Commission. The amendment to the 14-day vacathio legis was rejected at the committee meeting. This haste indicates that the government is aware of the dissatisfaction that farmers may have had over the long term to give up subsidies and credit bonuses. We still have a few more votes to vote on, and we still need to think about it. Of course, this project has advantages. We don't have a choice either. We need to think about it and we will decide during the vote on support for this project. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u410	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of Parliamentary Club Law and Justice vis-à-vis the Government's draft law on ratification of Council Decision (EU, Euratom) 2018/994 of 13 July 2018 amending the Act concerning the election of members of the European Parliament in direct universal elections, annexed to Council Decision 96/787/ECSC, EEC, Euratom of 20 September 1976. The proposal concerns the following changes in the elections to the European Parliament. And I will briefly present the main changes. The proposed law gives Member States the opportunity to vote in advance, to vote in correspondence, to vote electronically and to vote on the Internet. It also introduces a minimum time limit for the submission of candidates to the European Parliament if such a time limit is adopted in the country concerned, at least 3 weeks before the expected date of the elections to the European Parliament. It also gives the possibility to impose sanctions for voting more than once. It then points out that Member States have taken the necessary measures to enable their nationals residing in third countries to vote. The most recent major changes require the appointment of a contact authority responsible for the exchange of data with an appropriate equivalent in other countries. And the question is, what's all this for? All this in order to increase European political awareness, to increase electoral turnout - at least that is hope - and to take full advantage of the opportunities offered by technological advances. I want to say that the Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u411	Thank you very much. And I invite Mr Paweł Arndt to present the position of the Civic Platform-Citizen Coalition Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u412	Mrs. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! Ministers! We are discussing a draft act on ratification of the Council's decision amending the act concerning the election of the members of the European Parliament. Usually, ratification laws are not particularly emotional, they are not controversial. I think it is, and in this case, this bill does not arouse such emotions either. The law actually contains two articles. The first article authorises the President of the Republic of Poland to ratify the Council Decision. Article two refers to the entry into force of the Act. In fact, my previous speaker has already told us what the decision is. Indeed, this decision aims to encourage voters to take part in elections to the European Parliament. And here a number of facilitations have been introduced, particularly regarding correspondence voting or electronic voting. I want to explain here, because not everyone knows exactly the environmental law that this article. Article 129 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 refers to these claims: for the purchase of the property or part of it by the owner, for which the owner of the property may apply; for compensation for damage suffered, for the owner of the property; for the purchase of the property or part of it, or for compensation for which the perpetual user may apply. These situations are described in detail in this Article. Article 129 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, 2 and 3. All cases in this respect shall be settled by judicial procedure. Projects for which this restricted use area can be established are e.g. the establishment of waste water treatment plants, municipal waste dumps, composting plants, communication routes, as well as the creation of an airport. It should be stressed here that the nature of these compensation processes has changed considerably in the case of airports over recent years. While, in the past, owners or users have complained to public entities about compensation for the costs of sound insulation, there has been a change in the nature of these claims in recent years, as these are claims relating to the loss of value of the property due to the existence of the airport. As a result, the amount of compensation claimed is increasing dramatically. As we can read in the opinion of the Act, in 2013 4700 claims amounted to PLN 72 billion. Of course, these demands are always a bit exaggerated, but I give you this size because it speaks of the nature of the compensation to which public entities are exposed. The Court goes without saying that there is concern about public finances, the desire to reduce expenditure burdening the budget of the state or local government, and the will to pay all losses caused by the limitation of ownership and other property rights played a less important role. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u448	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I present a report on the draft parliamentary resolution on honoring the Secret Military Organisation of the Pomeranian Gryf, prints No. 3140 and 3188. Autumn 1939. Germany illegally incorporated the western and northwestern parts of the Republic of Poland into the Reich. The policy pursued by the occupier against the Polish population consisted primarily of the absolute violation of resistance, extermination and forced Germanization. Historians estimate that about 50,000 were killed by Germans in the autumn of 1939. Pomerania residents. In these circumstances, at the end of 1939, the Secret Military Organisation of Kashubski was established, the founders of which were Lt. Józef Dambek, Clement Bronk, Bronisław Brunka, Józef and Jan Gierszewski. In 1941, the organization was renamed the Secret Military Organisation of the Pomeranian Gryf, and this year Fr. Józef Wrycza, becoming the first President of the General Council of the Gryfa. Fr. Józef Wrycza was a true priest and a well-known independence activist in Pomerania in 1917 and 1920, a participant in the Wielkopolska Uprising and the Polish-Bolshevik War, and participated in the weddings of Poland and the Baltic, where during Mass. He gave a sermon. It is thanks to the great authority of Fr. Joseph's underground organization begins to grow stronger and more and more new members and associates appear. During the most active activity, the number of members of the griffin reached around 20 thousand. soldiers and supporters. The main activity of this Catholic organization focused primarily on self-defense - about 100 combat actions were carried out - training, propaganda, intelligence and diversional and sabotage actions. Effectively, they fought the occupation administration and the police, helped to hide from the wanted, supporting the morale of the inhabitants, published newspapers and leaflets, helped families murdered, rescued prisoners, warned civilians against arrests and planned expulsions. Mass arrests in 1943 inhibited the development of the Pomeranian Gryf Pomorskie, and after the arrest and murder in 1944, cf. Joseph Dambek's activity began to fade slowly. In the end, Gryf Pomorskie was dissolved by order of 21 March 1945. It is worth recalling a passage from the Idea Declaration that is well-defined by the objectives of the Secret Military Organisation To this end, we will strive for all the means at our disposal, to raise our conscious goals of the Pole - a soldier, criminal, disciplined and full consecration for the good of the general and homeland. In view of the above, and taking into account the activities of the members of the Secret Military Organisation, Gref Pomorskie, the committee welcomed the draft resolution, expressing its appreciation for the heroes fighting the German invader. Following the first reading and the examination of the draft resolution at its meeting on 30 January, the committee proposes that the draft resolution be adopted by the Sejm. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It comes from the arsenal of Soviet historical semantics. The autumn war campaign in 1939 was the first campaign launched in September 1939. The Second World War, not a separate and exclusively Polish defence war. The strongest confirmation of the fact that this campaign was part of World War II is the accession to war, although only formal or declarative, allies: France and the United Kingdom. The Third German Reich and the Soviet Union were the aggressors and perpetrators of this war. The concept of defence war was followed by Communist historians following Soviet historians to justify the thesis that World War II only broke out on June 22, 1941, at the time of the Third Reich's aggression against the Soviet Union. According to this concept, earlier military activities of the Soviet Union, including aggression towards Poland on 17 September 1939, supporting the aggression of Nazi Germany, were to be the introduction of the Third Reich and the Soviet Union of the world order. Therefore, in some sense, this wording weakens the pronunciation of this beautiful resolution, because the Gryf Pomorskie was a beautiful manifestation of Polish resistance against German aggression and honoring this organization should not constitute an opportunity for semantic manipulation and forgery of Polish history. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u464	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 31 January, the Committee on Culture and Media discussed a parliamentary draft resolution initiated by Mr Andrzej Melak on the celebration of the 100th anniversary of Ignacy Jan Paderewski's cabinet, print No 3180. High Izbo! This is another resolution within the entire cycle of celebrations of the 100th anniversary of regaining independence. I personally think that it is never too much of a reminder of the achievements of the fathers of our independence and the achievements of our predecessors in the Parliamentary benches, because it is a wonderful achievement, it is a subject of our admiration and I think it should also be a source of inspiration. Similarly, I believe that all the members of the committee are thinking, because the committee, following the introduction of cosmetic amendments to the draft, unanimously supported the text of the resolution, which I will allow me to present quickly. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the celebration of the 100th anniversary of Ignacy Jan Paderewski's office. Ignacy Jan Paderewski's office was created on 16 January 1919 as a result of an earlier agreement between Mr Józef Piłsudski and the President of the National Committee of Polish Roman Dmowski. At the head of the government was a politician whose merits were known internationally and who gave hope to reconcile a very diverse political scene at that time. It was the first Polish government after Poland regained its independence, to which all local government institutions in the former Kingdom of Poland, Galicia, Wielkopolska and Silesia Cieszyński submitted. It included both non-party ministers, including supporters of National Democracy, as well as representatives of the Polish Socialist Party, the Polish People's Party - Left and other groups. Ignacy Paderewski's office in the course of his activity contributed to the identification of a common Polish peace delegation for a peace conference in Paris. It was headed by Ignacy Paderewski - President of Ministers and, at the same time, Minister of Foreign Affairs and Roman Dmowski - President of the Polish National Committee. Thanks to the activities of the delegation, Poland gained international recognition as a sovereign European state and ally of the winning powers, as confirmed by the Treaty of Versailles on 28 June 1919. The government of Ignacy Paderewski led to democratic elections to the Legislative Sejm on 26 January 1919 and to local government in February 1919, the formation of the State Police, the expansion of state administration and local government, public education of all types, the Polish diplomatic service and other state institutions, as well as the start of reconstruction of the country's economy using market economy mechanisms. In recognition of the great merits of Ignacy Jan Paderewski's office, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland commemorates the 100th anniversary of his formation. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u467	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform will address Mrs Elżbieta Gapinska.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u468	Thank you very much, Marshal. I would like to remind you of the conditions under which Ignacy Jan Paderewski began ruling. The first government of Prime Minister Moraczewski in the first weeks had many successes, e.g. universal electoral law including women, an 8-hour day's work, the legality of trade unions and the right to strike, the appointment of labour inspection and sickness insurance, but with governance it did not work well. There was a wave of strikes and peasant speeches across the country. The workers took over the industrial plants, the landowners were thrown out of the manor houses, and the land was self-sacrificed. The situation was serious and the revolution was feared. Then Józef Piłsudski realized that he would not be able to persuade the Entents to acknowledge the Moraczewski government. His choice was made by Ignacy Paderewski, who has been involved in Polish politics since the beginning of the 20th century, becoming one of the main advocates of Polish independence. January 11th, I'll remind you, 1917. Paderewski handed over Paderewski's memorial to President Wilson, in which he called for the creation of the United States of Poland as a Federation State covering most of the area of the Republic of both Nations from before the partitions. Thanks to Ignace Paderewski, as Mr Paderewski said, the issue of Poland is in the famous 14 points of Wilson. Paderewski was to personally lead the interim government with a large group of partyless ministers. Some of them were supporters of National Democracy. Many tasks have been put before the new government. One of the first was to reach an agreement with the National Committee of Poland in Paris, and another was to hold elections to the Sejm planned for 23 January 1919. The new government managed to accomplish some of its tasks. One of his greatest successes was recognition in the international arena. I will remind you that this happened after the truce in Trevira and the ceasefire on the Polish-Ukrainian front in early 1919. On January 30, the first government of the Republic of Poland recognized the United States, then other countries. On 20 February 1919, Prime Minister Ignacy Paderewski gave an exposé. The next few days were supposed to show that the new government wasn't going to be easy. The country was destroyed by partitions and war, consisting of three parts of former partitions which were not compatible with one another in terms of economy, structure, administration or infrastructure. The huge problem was the budget in which there was no money, and because of the war, the citizens of the new state were unable to bear the burden of paying taxes. The Paderewski government has dealt with a number of reforms. One of the most important issues was the issue of agricultural reform, the enmity of the peasants. An attempt has also been made to adopt the Constitution. However, the collapse of the government stopped further work on the constitution, as well as on the introduction of controlled grain trade, which was intended to solve the issue of hunger in some parts of Poland. The Paderewski government was temporary. He had great successes in foreign policy, he calmed the state's mood, but it did not cloud the problems of the economic crisis, inflation and social unrest. After all the problems... [[♪ The bell ♪]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u500	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Committee on Culture and Media took place before 2 days of the parliamentary project and presented to the High Sejm a little bit changed in relation to the original text, but in its basic wording the same text of the resolution commemorating Kazimierz Wierzyński. 50 years ago, in London, exactly 13 February 1969, a few hours after the end of work on his last volume of poems, the poet died of heart. It is difficult to complement the creative path of this passionate patriot and zealous artist, singer of beauty and tradition of Christian faith, son of Kresów and exile from his homeland more symbolically. Kazimierz Wierzyński became famous in the interwar period. His debut with a poetic tome of spring and wine took place exactly 100 years ago, in 1919. Priced and known at that time, he became famous as a master of pure poetry in which the unity of language, image and internal experience was compared to a mystical experience. The young volumes of Wierzyński co-created the atmosphere of an independent homeland were a manifestation of vitality, energy and enthusiasm. Tom's tragic freedom paid tribute to the greatness of Józef Piłsudski's figure. Post-war emigration work Wierzyński is a mature hymn in honor of God's order, looking into human tragedy and suffering of the nation. From the poetry of Kazimierz Wierzyński comes one of the most beautiful metaphors of Poland's love and Polish language written in the 20th century. The poem of the Vistula and the island begins with the words "Poland lies over the Vistula." No one is a lonely island. We all know that. And for nothing. And then we read, "We feel moisture with our fingers." It's the Vistula. We can feel the roughness with our fingers. It's an island. And then the world ends with a cliff. Patriotism locked in the sterns of Kazimierz Wierzyński has power, contains tragic question marks, but is not sentimental. To this day, many Poles can say that there is a thrill on their backs when they hear or read one of the greatest poems by Kazimierz Wierzyński, written during World War II, entitled "Come down, spirit of power": "Come down, spirit of power/ (...) / Increase our strength/ Over brotherly graves, over the despair of orphans and widows (...) / This war we must win... this winter or in many years, (...) / We must fight, we must fight/ Until the last day. His poems of speech and earth and the fifth season of the year are regarded as the greatest masterpieces describing the value of the native language and the unique meaning of human fate, inscribed in the history of the nation. The poet was also the master of the description of the Polish landscape and the subtle painter of nature, and his role was described best in the poem Kazimierz Wierzyński has included his greatest achievements in nearly 20 volumes of poems so diverse that it would be possible to divide this work by several poets, and great poets. He was primarily an artist of the word. From his early years, however, he considered it his duty to participate actively in the struggle for the freedom of his country. As a student, he joined a conspiracy organization and joined the Eastern Legion during World War I. After his escape from Russian captivity, he co-founded the Polish Military Organization in Kiev. During the war with the Bolsheviks he was an officer of the Polish army and a war correspondent. During World War II and during the communist enslavement he organized cultural life in the country, visited Polish soldiers, as well as arrived with his poems and radio columns through Radio Free Europe to his country's compatriots. His words are well-known, cited for Stefan Żeromski, that if everyone left the Polish soldier, he would not be betrayed by Polish poetry. In 1968, he published a memorable volume of poems, the Black Polonaise, in which he made the biggest accusation of the communist system and claimed the truth about the Katyn crime. The black polonaise of the Communists never forgave him because he had an accusatory poem in it to his companion Wiesław, but also praise of the eternal love of Poland, over the dirt of the Polish People's Republic, in the work of the Night Homeland. High Seym! Kazimierz Wierzyński ruled the Polish word perfectly. By fascinated poetry researchers he was compared to Mickiewicz. His poems, especially the most beautiful post-war songs, the mentioned speech and earth, and the fifth season of the year, were combined with Mickiewiczowski's artistry, Pan Tadeusz. Because at the same time they are accessible in reception and are excellent in poetic art. Wierzyński touched, tangiblely loved and fertile, and rocky Polish land, fallen, decaying beech leaves, proud oak noises, soft-seeded moths, barley and sub-white. Maria Dłuska, one of the best experts in Polish poetry, wrote that Wierzyński has blood circulation in common with this land. Finally, I quote this poem, "Spoken and earth," the first verse: "They stole it from beech and birch," and then the Carpathian van carried it and then they put it in my ear, and we went together, speech and I, with a broad guest of a bright day/ And it seemed - the world would listen to us." Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u501	Thank you very much. The Sejm will hear three minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles in the discussion on this item. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Piotr Babinetz, the Law and Justice Club, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u502	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour - but also the joy - to present the position of the club Law and Justice towards the parliamentary draft resolution on the 100th anniversary of the debut and 50th anniversary of the death of Kazimierz Wierzyński. Kazimierz Wierzyński is a unique figure in the history of Polish literature. He was from Eastern Małopolska and for the rest of his life he remembered the beauty of those sites. In Drohobych his small debut took place in 1913 in the poem 'Hey', when, when, which appeared in the journal '1863' devoted to the January Uprising. In his youth he engaged in independence conspiracy in the ranks of Polish Shooting Teams and then in the Eastern Legion. The fight in the Austro-Hungarian army, to which he was incarcerated when he did not reach the brigade of Chief Józef Piłsudski, came to me to occupy a long Russian slavery. However, the period of the First World War ended in the conspiracy of the Polish Military Organization. When the Bolshevik raid came just after regaining independence, he joined the Polish Army. The right poetic debut of Wierzyński was a volume of poems He co-founded the most famous Polish literary group Skamander. His sports passion was very significant. He edited a magazine "Przegląd Sportowy" and in 1928 behind the volume "Olympic Laur" he received a gold medal at the 9th Summer Olympics literary competition. It is amazing, for example, to describe the performances of the Spanish goalkeeper Zamora and the reaction of fans in the poem "Match football." In later years, in subsequent volumes he referred to the fate of his homeland more and more tragic, and during World War II most of his family was murdered. Thus, his poetry had such a pronunciation, e.g. On September 1st, 1944, the National Army's solution, the Moscow process, they shouted freedom, the obituary. But in the poem "Night's Homeland," he expressed his confidence that Poland would survive the communist occupation: "Faithfulness of conscience/ Sense beyond defeat," "It was ours/It is and will remain." When Wierzyński died in exile, in an unbreakable environment, London news wrote: With the deepest sorrow in our hearts, we say goodbye to the poet of freedom, the creator of the incomparable, conscious of his art and his message, a faithful friend, a companion of works and fights, the best of us who left in unforgettable poems a testimony of our experiences and emotions. It can be said that Wierzyński's work format is just behind Zbigniew Herbert, who described him as a seismograph writing down the nation's contribution, and before Jack Kaczmarski, who devoted one of his texts to him. I believe that Wierzynski deserves an annual patronage, not just an occasional resolution. But it will be valuable if this commemoration of the poet contributes to the release of all his works. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u508	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Kazimierz Wierzyński is one of the most outstanding Polish writers of the 20th century, and at the same time an inconclusive and even full of contradictions - poet, playwright, sports journalist, radio columnist, co-founder of the Skamander poetic group, author of the outstanding biography of Fryderyk Chopin. Kazimierz Wierzyński was a semi-Austrian and became one of the largest Polish writers. From September 1939 until his death in 1969 he was in exile, but at this point we may wonder if he was not more concerned with Poland than Gombrowicz at all times. He devoted almost every single song to his country. He was well described by Zbigniew Herbert, saying that Wierzyński was like a seismograph writing down the nation's bottom. That's what happened. The creativity of this poet like no other reflected the mood of the Polish society. His poetic debut, volume spring and wine of 1919, was maintained in an optimistic convention. However, after the death of Józef Piłsudski in 1936, the collection of "Tragic Freedom" stands out with pessimism and concern for Poland and a clear fascination with the character of the Marshal. In later years Wierzyński noted further phrases in his work. His last volume of poems, the Black Polonaise, published in symbolic 1968, is full of references to the contemporary realities of the Polish People's Republic and references to the romantic myth of the artist fighting the tyranny of power. However, with all his love for Poland and Poland, Wierzyński noticed national shortcomings, such as excessive nationalism or a tendency to dispute, which he could stigmatize. Today, when we live in a divided society, in times of often Polish-Polish war, his works especially deserve to be reminded and understood. I therefore hope that through this resolution we will be able to... that this resolution will become an excuse for e.g. the issue of programs about Wierzyński on public television or the release by the Ministry of Culture of the collection containing its most important works. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Our club will support this draft resolution. Thank you. [[Applause]]

This turned out to be very important in 1920, when during the war with Russia, Soviet Witos encouraged and encouraged the peasants to participate actively in the fight for Poland, to resist Russian propaganda. He thought that if you borrow money, then you have to give it back, and he thought it was very important to keep an eye on the budget in these difficult times. These assumptions were the basis for easier economic reforms. Witos lost his last premiere after the May bombing. Then the trial of many democratic opposition activists took place, Witos was convicted in the Brest trial. After he left the prison, he left Poland, he was in exile. He returned to Poland during the German invasion of Poland, at the beginning of the war. He was arrested by the Germans, but refused any cooperation with them and was faithful to his ideals to the end, not subject to any influence. All along the political path he began, he was faithful to all principles for the rest of his life. The Commission submitted to the government in Warsaw on 11 November 1918 after the surrender of the central states. Vincent Witos has been actively involved in actions aimed at restoring the state, defending its borders and unifying it. Since 1919, he has been a Member of the Polish Parliament, three times headed by the Polish Government, contributing to the economic and social stability of Poland. In 1930, the Sanatorium authorities accused him of the so-called. Brest trial and sentenced to a year and a half in prison. Since 1933, he has been in exile in Czechoslovakia, where after the German troops entered Poland, he returned to Poland and called for the defence of his homeland. Right after the start of World War II, he was interned by the Germans. He received a proposal to set up a collaborating government that he strongly rejected. From 28 February 1941, under constant supervision of the Gestapo, he stayed in Wierzchosławice. After the war ended, he became involved in the reconstruction of the people's movement. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u538	Dear Marshal, On behalf of the PSL-UED Club, I support the report of the committee on the Government's draft law on ratification of the Agreement between the Republic of Poland and the Kingdom of Denmark on the Baltic Pipe project signed in Katowice on 11 December 2018 (print No 3194).
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u539	We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 55 of the agenda: First reading of the draft Act on amendment of the Commercial Companies Code Act presented by the President of the Republic of Poland (Print No 3142). I ask the Deputy Head of the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland of Mr.Paweł Mucha to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u540	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the President of the Republic of Poland Mr Andrzej Duda a bill amending the Commercial Companies Code Act. A detailed argument containing the justification of the bill presented is contained in the application submitted by the President, Parliamentary Printing No. 3142. For this reason, I will limit myself only to making the most important arguments that led to the President's presentation with the initiative discussed today. As you know, a limited liability company in Poland is the most frequently chosen legal form of the company enabling it to conduct business activity. A number of responsibilities are linked to its functioning. One of them is the need to hold an annual meeting of shareholders to approve the company's activity reports or to grant discharge to the members of the Management Board. In many other matters relating to the company's current business, it is also necessary to convene a meeting of shareholders in order to adopt the relevant resolutions. As practice shows, the organisation of a meeting of shareholders is often a technical and organisational problem. Partners in a large number of limited liability companies are foreign persons, both physical and legal. If the shareholder is a foreign entity or a foreigner, or if at least one of the partners is present on a daily basis outside Poland, the holding of the shareholders is a challenge for both the company and the partners. Consequently, the possibility of holding a meeting of shareholders using electronic means of communication, i.e. also over the Internet, it would certainly be an attractive alternative for entrepreneurs. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In the current state of law, the Commercial Companies Code provides that, as a general rule, the shareholders of a limited liability company are held at the company's registered office, meaning the place where the company's registered office is located. By way of exception, if the partnership agreement or all the partners so agree in writing, it is possible to hold a meeting of the shareholders elsewhere in the territory of the Republic of Poland. At the same time, many of the existing legal solutions are a more or less effective attempt to remedy the situation of the inability to hold shareholders' meetings using electronic means. Currently, with respect to limited liability companies, it is only possible to adopt shareholders' resolutions via the Internet when the company was established in the online S24 system. In addition, the adoption of a resolution in this manner requires the participation of all shareholders. As a result, the existing solution excludes in practice the possibility for shareholders to adopt resolutions electronically in the case of a large majority of limited liability companies. In legal literature, most posts do not recognise the admissibility of electronic meetings of shareholders. The commentators in the position of inadmissibility of the electronic meetings of the shareholders are prohibited from holding such meetings in the Article. 234 § 2 Codek of commercial companies which are required to hold a meeting of shareholders in the territory of the Republic of Poland. It is therefore entirely appropriate to introduce a clear statement by the legislator allowing the general assembly of shareholders using electronic means of communication. The intention of the submitted project is to introduce a broad understanding of the institutions of electronic meetings of shareholders. In the literature of the subject, the conduct of meetings in the form of teleconferences or video conferencing shall be distinguished. The assembly consists of transmitting by electronic means the meetings held at the place specified in the articles of association to other places where the partners are gathered. On the other hand, the videoconference is a kind of electronic connection between partners staying in different locations. A partner may be in a place other than the place of the meeting, but at a place indicated by the company. In the case of a videoconference, the partner may join it from any place having access to electronic communications used to conduct a videoconference. The project leaves the choice of the term in which the above mentioned above. forms are to be held by electronic meetings, the freedom of shareholders expressed in the relevant provisions in the articles of association. At the same time, despite the adoption of an electronic form of assembly, this does not exempt the company from determining the place where the managing partner meeting, together with the minutes and possible bodies of the company, will physically arrive. It should be pointed out that, in the current legal state, it is possible to adopt resolutions using means of direct distance communication in the case of general meetings of shareholders and supervisory boards of both limited liability companies and public limited liability companies. After analysing the functioning of similar regulations in other European Union countries, it should be pointed out that the holding of electronic meetings of shareholders is acceptable in many European counterparts of the Polish limited liability company. For example, it is worth mentioning the German company GmbH at this point. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As regards the scope of the proposed solution, it should be pointed out that it is largely modelled on the rules currently in force in the Commercial Companies Code governing the organisation of general meetings of shareholders, in particular the regulation of the Article. 406 Commercial Companies Code, which provides that the statutes of a public limited liability company may allow participation in a general meeting using electronic means of communication. In order to effectively implement the proposed regulation, it is proposed to add an Article. 234 Commercial Companies Code providing for the possibility to hold meetings of shareholders by electronic means. Given the digital form of resolutions that are taken at the electronic meeting of shareholders, I would like to say that according to the draft, the minutes of the meeting of shareholders held electronically constitute an audio-video record. The record of audio-video enables compliance with the requirements laid down in the Commercial Companies Code, according to which the protocol should contain information on the correctness of convening the shareholders' meeting and its ability to adopt resolutions, listing the adopted resolutions, the number of votes cast for each resolution and the objections raised. With regard to e.g. reporting obligations, the electronic minutes of the shareholders' meeting will require appropriate transcription in order to obtain a material medium of resolutions. Resolutions adopted at the electronic meeting of shareholders will be included in the minutes book in the form of transcription of the electronic meeting of shareholders. At the same time, evidence of the convening of the shareholders' meeting by the Management Board and the audio-video record of the shareholders' meeting will be attached to the transcription protocol book. In selecting an electronic meeting of shareholders, it is not necessary to attach a list of attendance with the signatures of the participants in the meeting of shareholders, because the fact of the presence or absence of the partner will be provided by the record of audio-video as a protocol from the meeting of shareholders. The transcription of the electronic meeting of shareholders should include, in particular, information on the resolutions adopted and the objections raised by the shareholders at the electronic meeting. As in the case of resolutions adopted under the procedure laid down in Article. 240 Commercial Companies Code, transcription of the protocol containing information about adopted resolutions will be subject to notification in the electronic system of the registry court, among others. in the case of approval of the financial statements or of a resolution on profit-sharing or loss-covering. Security of resolutions in this form will be ensured, among others. the actual possibility for each partner to record a meeting of shareholders. In this way, it will be possible for the partner to prove that he had lodged an objection at the shareholders' meeting, which would enable action to be taken to annul or repeal the resolution. The draft amendment also refers to the issue of secret voting, indicating that it is permissible to adopt personal resolutions at meetings of shareholders held by electronic means, and for obvious reasons the regulation of the Article will not apply. 247 § 2 of the Commercial Companies Code. At this point, it is necessary to stress that the new law does not impose an obligation on limited liability companies to organise a meeting of shareholders by electronic means, but gives rise to the right to take such a decision. Each company could decide on its own whether and how to benefit from the statutory regulation. The provision set out in this amendment, as well as the rules on the electronic holding of general meetings of shareholders, is technologically neutral, i.e. does not impose any specific technical solutions, which is an advantage and indicates the possibility of adapting the application of these provisions to changing circumstances and technical progress. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In the light of the above considerations, it is appropriate to consider the legislative intervention aimed at amending and clarifying the rules relating to the arrangements for holding meetings of members of limited liability companies using electronic means of communication to be fully justified. I would like to point out that the project submitted is the result of the work of the Office of the National Council for Development, Local Government and Civil Initiatives in the Chancellery of the President of the Republic of Poland. It was created in cooperation with the Centre im. Adam Smith, whose representatives, with the president of the centre, are present in the Chamber of the High House. Thank you for this initiative, which was reported by the business community. The project was also consulted as part of the work of the National Development Council. Taking all the above into account, on behalf of the President of the Republic of Poland, I would like to ask for a favourable consideration of the proposed bill amending the Commercial Companies Code Act and to undertake further legislative work on the draft. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u560	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Committee on Culture and Media about the parliamentary draft resolution commemorating the patron Jan Olszewski - President of the Council of Ministers of the Republic of Poland. The Commission shall, after first reading and considering this draft resolution at its meeting on 21 February 2019, request the adoption of the draft as follows: The resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemorating the patron of Jan Olszewski - the President of the Council of Ministers of the Republic of Poland. On February 7, 2019 Jan Ferdinand Olszewski - lawyer, activist of independence opposition, politician died. He was born on 20 August 1930 in Warsaw in a family of railwaymen, politically connected with the traditions of the independence of the socialist movement. He grew in an atmosphere of respect for independent Poland, which he remained faithful to for the rest of his life. During World War II he was a soldier of the Grey Troops. He fought in the Warsaw Uprising. Since 1956 he has been actively involved in Polish break-ups as a member of the Krzywy Circle Club and editor of the Simply. From the 1960s to the end of the Polish People's Republic he was a defender in political trials. He defended students, opposition activists repressed by the communist authorities, people of Solidarity in years of martial law, among others. in trials against strike organizers and underground publishers. He was one of the auxiliary prosecutors in the Toruń trial, representing the family of the murdered bl. Fr. George Popieluszko. In 1968 and 1970, because of his taking up the defence of students arrested during the March demonstrations in Warsaw, he had a suspended right to pursue the profession of lawyer. In December 1975 he became a co-author and signatory of Letter 59 - an appeal to the PRL Sejm containing protest against the proposed changes in the Constitution of the Polish People's Republic limiting the sovereignty of Poland. In 1976 he was among the founders of the Polish Independence Agreement and participated in the establishment of the Committee for the Defence of Workers. In September 1980, he joined the organization of the Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union (Solidarity), was an advisor to the National Commission (Solidarity) and one of the authors of its first statute. After the introduction of the martial law he led on behalf of the secretary-general of the Polish Bishops' Conference. Bronisław Dąbrowski talks with representatives of the authorities about the release of prisoners from internment camps. In the first fully democratic elections to the Sejm in 1991, he was appointed Member of the Sejm for the first term of office. On December 6, 1991, the Sejm appointed him President of the Council of Ministers. He was the first Prime Minister of the government appointed by the Sejm in the post-war history of Poland elected in free elections. As President of the Council of Ministers, he made an international effort to bring Poland into NATO and the European Communities, while opposing the country's dependence on Russia. At the request of the Sejm, the government of Jan Olszewski - implementing the illustrious resolution - provided Parliament with a list of nearly 70 people performing important state functions, registered as agents of the civil security apparatus in the times of communist Poland. This attempt at judging was, among other things, the reason for his government's dismissal. The decision of the Sejm during the so-called. the night shift from 4 to 5 June 1992, on the anniversary of the 1989 elections, Jan Olszewski was deprived of the position of President of the Council of Ministers. He said from the Seym stand: 'When I included my functions [...] I knew that we would come to build a new system of democratic power in Poland, a new system, a new, third, our Polish Republic. [...] I would like to leave, namely, when this building leaves, when it ends for me this, I do not hide, [...] a terribly distressing time that when it finally ends, I will be able to normally go out on the streets of this city and look people in the eye. And this to you, Members, Members, I wish you after this vote." He co-authored a civil draft of the 1994 Constitution, which was signed in a short time by 2 million Poles. After the presidential election in 1995, in which he won fourth place, he founded a new political party of the Movement for Reconstruction of Poland. He was an advisor to the President of the Republic of Poland Lech Kaczyński. He was working to liquidate the Military Information Services. On May 3, 2009, he was awarded the Order of the White Eagle. For the rest of his life, Prime Minister Jan Olszewski remained faithful to the ideals of independent, legal and democratic Poland. He was a man of honor, full of dignity, modesty and honesty. He was well registered in the history of the Polish nation as a patriot, statesman and role model. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to Him. Hello His memory! Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u562	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club a motion for a resolution commemorating Prime Minister Jan Olszewski. Sleep. He was an oppositionist for many years. On the pages of history he signed up as a steadfast politician called John the Great by his friends. He defended political prisoners, never took money for it. He was the accuser of the auxiliary chaplain of the Blessed Fr. George Popieluszko. At every stage of his life, he was distinguished by the beautiful attitude of a modest, honest man. And of such an attitude, such a man should remember the next generations. He often put his career and his life at stake. He had no doubt, like us, that everything he did was for the good of Poland. We know that in the last years of his life he has asked that democratic Poland be able to join and work with politicians over divisions for the sake of our beloved homeland. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u574	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen of the Ministers! High Izbo! I would like to present a report to the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies on the Government's draft law amending the telecoms law and certain other laws, prints Nos 3116 and 3156. It should be stressed that this is a particularly important and important project primarily because the need to amend the telecoms law results directly from the European Commission's proceedings concerning the incorrect application of EU directives. The European Commission has accused the Polish side of incorrect implementation in the national legal order of art. Article 14 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Authorisation Directive on the obligation to consult interested parties on intended changes to frequency reservation and raised doubts as to the rules governing selection procedures for frequency reservation by the President of the Office for Electronic Communications. The European Commission's reservations relate in particular to the non-binding nature of auctions and the lack of real financial consequences for an entity that has abandoned its previously quoted frequency reservations. Once again, I would like to stress that these shortcomings in the auction procedure, also indicated by the European Commission, had a direct impact on the course of the only 800 MHz 2015 auction in Poland, in which one of the operators artificially raised the price and finally resigned from the reservation. The aim of the presented project is therefore primarily to improve the rules governing selective procedures for the reservation of frequencies, which will avoid a situation where very high penalties for the incorrect transposition into Polish law of the provisions of the EU Directive will be imposed on Poland. At the same time, the project gives the government the opportunity to set a timetable for the distribution of frequencies, which is a tool allowing enforcement from the Office of Electronic Communications of activities to which Poland is obliged as a Member State of the European Union. In the current state of law, the government does not have any tools to oblige the President of the Office of Electronic Communications to take action to fulfil international obligations. As a result, in many areas of the UKE President's activity there is a delay today, which will adversely affect the development of the telecommunications market and thus also the economic development of our country. Commission for Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technology at its meeting on 19 February considered the amendments tabled during the second reading of the project. [[But it's already been done.]] Amendment 2 was accepted in the vote and amendments 1, 3, 4, and 5 were rejected. On behalf of the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies, I recommend the adoption of Amendment 2, as well as the adoption of the entire draft law amending the telecoms law and certain other laws, together with the amendment. Thank you. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u744	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 18 January this year. He died in Opole, Fr. Dr. Alojzy Sitek. Sleep. Fr. Alojzy Sitek was born on 29 January 1929 in Cisówka - now it is a district of Jastrzębia-Zdrój - as a fifth child with eight siblings. After the outbreak of World War II, the father was mobilized, so the whole family went to the East: first to Trembola in the district of Ternopil and then to Lviv. After the Red Army occupied the area, they returned to Silesia and moved to Pszów. In this town, Alojzy Sitek graduated from primary school in 1943. He could not continue his studies because of the war, so he took up work in the mine of Anna in Pszów. After the end of the war in 1945 and the return from the German father's captivity, he began to study in a general secondary school in Rybnik. After his father's transfer to Rybnik, where he was to work as a teacher, Alojza Sitek continued to study in a general secondary school in Raciborz, and after his graduation he enrolled in high school. He completed them and obtained a certificate of maturity in 1949. Immediately after his graduation, he joined a seminary in Opole-Nysa and prepared for the priesthood. He received priestly ordination from Bishop Zdzisław Goliński in the Opole Cathedral on 20 June 1954. As a vicar in the parish of St. Our Lady of God's Help of the Faithful in Kluczbork worked for 6 years. In 1960 he became a notary of the Opole curia, which he served as a notary for 10 years, in parallel with his pastoral duties. In 1970 he became chancellor of the diocesan curia and held this responsible office for almost 27 years. Between 1997 and 2004, he was the director of the diocesan archive and at the same time an employee of Radio Plus Opole, today's Radio Doxy. After his retirement in 2004, he moved to the home of retired priests, where he died on 18 January 2004. 11 days before the 90th birthday. Since September 1980 he has been a close collaborator of Opole structures, participants of meetings, celebrations organized by the opposition throughout the diocese, then with Bytom, Zabrzem and Gliwice. After 13 December 1982, until 1989, he was the organizer and coordinator of the works of the Opole Primate Committee for the Assistance of Liberty Deprived Persons and their Families. He determined the fate of the interned, provided material, financial assistance, which he had paid against the fines he had tried, and provided assistance. He dealt with legal aid: he directed to trusted lawyers, paid their fees. He supported spiritually detained and their families. He was, along with Fr. Paul the Puddle and Fr. bp. Anton Adamiuk, an observer in court proceedings. I'm with Bishop. Anton Adamiuk visited the interned, e.g. In the center, he intervened in the case of sick leave. He was a co-organizer of Holy Masses for his homeland and preacher of patriotic sermons. In 1983 he was the initiator of the meeting of former political prisoners with John Paul II during his pilgrimage to St. Anna in Opole. Many times interrogated on behalf of the curia, he taught deacons how to behave in dealings with the SB. In 1986 he defended his doctoral thesis at the Papal Theological Faculty in Wrocław. Fr. Alojzy Sitek is also the author of historical works entitled: The problem of taking over the Evangelical churches by Catholics in Silesia Opole after the Second World War, and the organization and directions of the Apostolic Administration Curia of Silesia Opole in the years 1945-1956, as well as numerous memories. In 2005, he was awarded the honorary title of the citizen of Opole. Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-21-sejm-77-4.u754	Thank you, Marshal. High Izbo! The Middle East summit has recently ended in Poland, which, as it turned out, was the summit against Iran. We have learned that it is about preparing a war against this country. But we also learned another thing about this summit. The Secretary of State, Mike Pompeo, made a very clear request to the Polish Government to draw up special legislation to return the so-called. non-fallible property from the Holocaust era. Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately, the case comes back like a boomerang, because about a year ago the United States Congress, first the Senate and then the House of Representatives, adopted Law 447, which concerns the so-called. property without heirs, which is to be the subject of restitution in the countries to which it will refer, inter alia. to Poland. It must be recalled once again that this kind of law in Poland does not exist, because according to Polish law property without heirs is transferred to the property of the State Treasury and only special legislation, unique, contrary to our legal order, would have to change our law in such a way as to enable such restitution. It should also be recalled once again that we have an international agreement between Poland and the United States of America from 1960, which settled the issue of compensation for the property of American citizens, which has been nationalised in Poland or in any other way expropriated. In other words, those demands, which were once again made by Mike Pompeo, which stem directly from Law 447, have no grounds and there is no basis for creating specific legislation in this direction. It is also necessary to mention the agreement between Israel and the Government of the Federal Republic of Germany of 1952, in which the Government of the German Federal Republic committed itself to compensate Israel for the property without heirs left in those areas occupied by the Third Reich. Unfortunately, once again, the Polish government ignored these issues, considered that there is no problem, that it does not concern Poland, and once again probably today, if I remember correctly, Ambassador Mosbacher repeated again that Poland must unfortunately adopt such legislation. Therefore, we warned about a year ago that this might end this way, that there would be further steps that this process would proceed. Unfortunately, the Polish government ignored this and believes that the problem does not exist. But unfortunately, it is consistently implemented by the American side and it needs to be put to an end. Therefore, I strongly urge the Polish government to finally say to the American side clearly and definitively that, firstly, this issue was regulated on the basis of the 1960 agreement, secondly, there is an agreement between Israel and the German Federal Republic of 1952, and thirdly, Poland will not change legislation in such a way as to create exceptional, unusual rules concerning only a certain group that would gain from the fact that Polish citizens lost their lives - because they were Polish citizens - as a result of the actions of an enemy state, as a result of the actions of the Third Reich. I remind you once again: they were Polish citizens and it is their business, and it is Poland that has the most to say in this matter, we have lost the most. In short, it must be said out loud that in Poland the Act 477 will not be implemented, and this is what I call the Government of the Republic of Poland. Thank you.
